# Deviation log — UI system handoff 2026-09-06

Any page-local deviation from the handoff (`README.md` + `Caros Ledge UI System.dc.html`)
belongs here, not in a page's stylesheet (README "Overview"). Includes cases where a value
the design shows does not exist in the app's data yet and is rendered via the Absence
convention instead.

## RULINGS (operator, via Claude Design, 2026-09-07)

Design rulings received from the operator (via Claude Design) on 2026-09-07, binding on every UI
lane. Copied verbatim (per the dispatch that carried them) into this fold.

R1. Auth copy: keep the app's current approved wording inside artboard 16's layout, inputs and
buttons; the artboard's headline and paragraph are placeholder.

R2. Nav footer: two unlabelled rows below a divider, Account (workspace name right-aligned) and
Admin (OWNER badge); no 'Operator' heading.

R3. Detail summary depths: two ('Summary | Full brief'), beside the S1..Sn section index; the old
third depth was the dead span.

R4. Breadcrumb on details: 'VOL IV . NO. 36 . <Surface> / <Jurisdiction> / N of M in <Band>'; the
last segment is live place-keeping from the list-position contract, not decorative. Command-bar
placeholder on details is scoped ('Ask about this regulation' / signal / finding / profile).

R5. Detail action row: Export brief (primary ink) . Share . Watch (star filled when watched) . +
Tag, all visible on every detail header; the ... menu holds only rare and destructive items.

R6. Workspace tags: pill with 6 px square ink dot, #F5F2EE fill, rgba(0,0,0,.14) border, 11.5px/600,
removable x; shown under the detail title, on the list row second line, as a 'Workspace tags' facet
group with live counts, and as the source for saved views. The + Tag popover: 280 px, anchored below
the trigger, left-aligned; white, radius 10, 1px rgba(0,0,0,.12), card shadow, no band rule; 40 px
input row with the search glyph 15 px #7A6E6C, 13 px text, bottom border rgba(0,0,0,.08); 40 px rows,
12 px padding, pill + right-aligned muted 11 px count, hover #FAFAF8, applied tags show a check in ink
instead of the count; max height 320 px scrolling; footer 'Create "<typed>"' 12.5px/600 with the pill
preview when no exact match, top border rgba(0,0,0,.08); multi-select, stays open, arrows move, Enter
applies/creates, Esc closes, Backspace on empty input removes the last applied tag; applying updates
the detail tag row and rail facet counts immediately. Data: migration, workspace-scoped RLS like the
other workspace tables, tag names unique per workspace case-insensitively, deleting a tag deletes its
links (never soft-hides).

R7. Features not in the 17 artboards: leave exactly as is and list them; never remove or restyle
without a ruling. Rulings so far: Regulations row priority retag + Dismiss live in the row's ... menu;
the Dismissed stash stays as a collapsed disclosure at the card foot; Operations by-state sub-list
stays; the PERF-12 virtualized remainder stays.

R8. Community room feed: image 12's thread table is the room index, not the room. A thread opens as
its own page in the standard frame (masthead with breadcrumb Community / <room> / <thread title>,
content column at <=72ch, rail). Inside it a post is a FactCard: sourced variant when it carries
citations, dashed inference variant when it does not; verifier sign-off is a tier square plus a
small-caps attribution line in the card foot (same family as a source's tier, not a new badge); the
composer is one card at the foot of the thread. The compact table stays exactly as drawn; the feed
lives one level down. NEEDS AN ARTBOARD (queued by Claude Design): do not build the thread page until
it arrives; keep CommunityRooms' feed as is meanwhile.

R9. Settings sub-nav: the five second-level items (General / Saved searches / Data & supersessions /
Archive / Help) become the section index, reusing the sticky S1 . S2 . S3 SectionIndex from the
detail architecture: a horizontal row of section links directly under the Account tab row,
left-aligned, active in ink; the five become anchored sections in one scrolling page, not five
routes. Archive's viewer stays a page under Data; Help stays a route if it already is one (link it,
do not nest it). One tab row on the page. Can be built now from existing parts (no new component, no
artboard needed).

R10. Mobile 390 and tablet 1024: not designed; no responsive rules are invented; they arrive as
artboards (queue: overlays, then mobile 390, then community thread and the settings section index
artboards).

| 2026-09-07 | Environment: rendering-guard smoke navigation vs. the session's egress proxy | The rendering guard's UX smoke harness (`.discipline/rendering/smoke/harness.mjs`) navigates each mounted component to a same-origin fake URL (`https://smoke-guard.internal/`) intercepted via `page.route` before any real network call — this is the harness's own documented $0/no-network design. In this container, Chromium's system HTTPS_PROXY (`http://127.0.0.1:38777`, the CCR agent proxy) sends the initial CONNECT for that fake host to the proxy before Playwright's route interception can virtualize it; the proxy correctly denies it (org policy, not a real destination) and the navigation then hangs rather than failing fast, stalling the whole guard run. Fix: add `smoke-guard.internal` to `NO_PROXY`/`no_proxy` before invoking Chromium (`export NO_PROXY="$NO_PROXY,smoke-guard.internal"`), which lets Chromium attempt a direct (unproxied) connection that Playwright's interception then virtualizes as designed — confirmed: `detail-surfaces-smoke.mjs` alone went from an indefinite hang to 16/16 checks passing in ~12s, and the full `run-rendering-guard.mjs` then completed clean (0 unaccounted failures; only the pre-existing dated 375px list-page/map exemptions). No code in `.discipline/` was changed — this is an invocation-environment note only. | Guard mechanics are correct; the container's proxy config needed one extra `NO_PROXY` entry to let local-only interception work as designed. | 2026-09-08 | Audit harness — capture-compose-page.mjs silently truncated any mount taller than 1400px | [CONFIRMED, tested this session] `AppShell`'s frame is `height:100vh` with its OWN internal scroll (the nav card and content column scroll independently inside that fixed box) — the document `<body>` never grows past the viewport. `capture-compose-page.mjs`'s original `page.screenshot({fullPage:true})` measures body scroll height for its capture bounds, which stays at the 1400px viewport regardless of how much real content the mount has, so any compose-* mount taller than 1400px was captured with everything past that point silently cut off — no error, no warning, just a shorter-than-real PNG. First caught on `compose-settings` (real content ~4106px — the 40-sector grid alone runs past 4000px), then found to have ALSO already been silently truncating `compose-admin` (real content 1665px, cutting off the entire Organizations card — see the row above) even though `compose-admin`'s own audit spec had been passing throughout, because the spec's DOM query never depended on the screenshot or the viewport at all (`querySelectorAll` sees off-screen/unscrolled elements just fine) — only the visual side-by-side comparison this lane's own METHOD depends on was silently wrong. Fixed: the script now measures the mounted `[data-audit]` element's real height via `getBoundingClientRect()` and grows the viewport to fit before shooting a plain (non-fullPage) screenshot. Map/Community/Account's own real content all measured under 1400px (re-verified this session), so their existing side-by-side PNGs are unaffected; Admin's and Settings's were recaptured. | Read `capture-compose-page.mjs` and `AppShell.tsx`'s frame CSS this session; measured every compose-* mount's real content height directly. | lane compose-other, 2026-09-08 — a screenshot-truncation bug that masked a real composition defect (Admin's missing Organizations card); worth a standing reminder for any future full-page composition mount to re-check this before trusting a "no MISMATCH" spec result as proof the visual capture is complete. |

| 2026-09-08 | Admin (13), "Re-tier" has no in-place tier-write endpoint | [CONFIRMED, source-read this session] `/api/admin/sources/commit-tier-change` returns 409 for `kind:"provisional"` and directs callers to `/promote` (its own header says promotion owns provisional tier assignment). Without leaflet's own rules, nothing gives `.leaflet-pane` / `.leaflet-marker-icon` `position:absolute`, so every marker laid out in normal document flow at y ~= 3595 while the 420px card sits at y ~= 448, and the canvas photographed empty. Fixed in the HARNESS, not the product: `mounts.mjs` gains a `styleFiles` field and a `mountExtraCss(mount)` reader, honoured by both `run-audit.mjs` and `capture-compose-page.mjs`, pointed at `fsi-app/node_modules/leaflet/dist/leaflet.css`. leaflet is already an app dependency, nothing is fetched, and this is the same technique `.discipline/rendering/smoke/map-smoke.mjs` has used since lane uimapcomm. Proven by attack: putting `styleFiles: []` back turns the new geometric boundsCheck row MISMATCH ("extends outside its container") and nothing else. | dc.html id="p10"; the mount's own alias table; a scripted DOM measurement of `.leaflet-marker-icon` rects with and without the stylesheet. | lane map60, 2026-09-08 |

The spec asserts the frame artboard 16 DOES draw and /signup shares with /login, plus the page's own real fields; it explicitly does not claim artboard authority for signup-only content the artboard never draws. | 2026-09-08 | Artboard 04, NEXT DATA DROPS: a date that painted over its neighbour | [CONFIRMED, measured] Lane lists60 saw the two-digit date wrap with "10" orphaned and fixed it with `white-space: nowrap`. That traded the wrap for a 6px OVERFLOW: the track was a hard 70px, "Thu Sep 10" measures 75.7px, and every box up to the card is `overflow: visible`, so the glyphs painted into the producer-name column. clientWidth 70, scrollWidth 76. The track is `minmax(70px, auto)` now, so a single-digit date still resolves to EXACTLY the artboard's 70px and only a two-digit date grows, with the name column at `minmax(0, 1fr)` yielding the pixels. | Fold, visual pass; the operator's 2026-09-07 standing order on overflow |
| 2026-09-08 | Harness lesson: a bounds check cannot see TEXT overflow | [REFUTED, by attack] The row above was first guarded with a `boundsCheck` over the drop row's cells. Restoring the hard 70px left it MATCH, because a bounds check compares element RECTS and the span's rect stays 70px while its text paints outside it. The refuted check was REMOVED rather than left standing as an unproven guard, and replaced with the resolved track list, which does turn MISMATCH under the same attack. Recorded because the next lane guarding a spill will reach for the same wrong tool. | Fold, rule 15 (a guard is proven by attack, not by presence) |
| 2026-09-08 | Test harness, `AccountPrimitives.npmtest.mjs`'s 2200-character window | [CONFIRMED] The fold's own defect. `slice()` read a flat 2200-character window from `export function AccountCard`; settings60 and admin60 each added a comment inside that function and together pushed the head meta's `fontWeight: 600` to offset 2275. The component was correct and the assertion still described it correctly; a green test went red on prose alone. The window now ends at the next top-level `export`. | Fold |
| 2026-09-08 | Account (14) rail, MEMBER SINCE wraps to two lines: NOT FIXED, decision-ready | [CONFIRMED, measured, DELIVERED FOR A RULING] The rail's 2x2 stat card renders "Nov 2025" over two lines where the artboard sets its date on one. Root cause measured, not guessed: the grid is the artboard's own `1fr 1fr`, but a grid item's default `min-width: auto` lets "Enterprise" (one unbreakable word, max-content 144.2px) claim its whole track, leaving 107.8px for a value needing 134px. The card is 300px, its content box 266, so the two tracks share 252px. `min-width: 0` gives the artboard's intended 126px each, but then "Enterprise" at 144.2px overflows a 126px track, which is worse. Dropping the `.04em` letter-spacing the artboard does NOT declare on this value brings the pair to 126/134, which fits MEMBER SINCE and still leaves PLAN 8px over. The artboard's own card therefore cannot hold its own "Enterprise" value at its own Anton 26px in its own 300px rail (`minmax(0,1fr) 300px`, verbatim from dc.html p14, the same width the build uses). Every remaining fix is a change to the SHARED `StatBlock`'s value type, which AdminDashboard and DashboardBrief also render and which admin-stat-tiles.json and bandtile.json both measure. That is not a call a fold lane makes unilaterally. Not fixed here; stated with its numbers so the ruling can be made once, for all three surfaces. | Fold, rule 13 (decision-ready where a ruling blocks execution) |
| 2026-09-08 | DEVIATION-LOG, lane details60's section heading | [CONFIRMED] details60 appended its rows with no heading of its own, so after the chronological union they sat under lane admin60's table and read as admin rows. A heading was restored at the fold. The rows themselves are the lane's, unchanged. | Fold |

## Lane TAGS-401 (train 61, production-defect lane), 2026-09-08

This lane changed no geometry, no colour and no type. It is here because two of its rows are
decisions a later lane would otherwise re-litigate, and one is a deliberate non-fix.

| Date | Page / deviation | Detail | Ruled by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-08 | Every detail page, the `workspace tags` row rendered its label with no pills: NOT a design gap | [CONFIRMED, production] The 2026-09-08 click-through audit read this as a visual finding. It is not. `GET /api/workspace/tags` returned 401 on every signed-in page load (23 in three hours), because `src/lib/tags/client.ts` sent `credentials: "include"` and `requireAuth` reads the `Authorization` header and nothing else. `DetailTagRow` was rendering its Absence state correctly against an empty tag list. Nothing about the row's design was wrong, so nothing about it changed; the API now answers and the pills appear. Recorded so the row is never "fixed" by restyling it. | Lane, on evidence |
| 2026-09-08 | The rendering guard's API fixtures answer `**/api/**` without inspecting the request: kept, with ONE exception | [CONFIRMED, attack-verified] Every existing `apiRoutes` fixture fulfils any `/api/` call with a canned body. That is right for a layout measurement and wrong for an auth contract, and it is why a feature that sent no credentials at all photographed identically to a correct one. Rather than change the shared fixture (which would make every unrelated mount carry an auth concern), ONE spec, `workspace-tags-smoke.mjs`, replicates requireAuth's contract and refuses an unauthenticated request. Verified by attack in both directions: with the pre-fix client in place the spec reports the production symptom verbatim, including the tag row's text as `⌕workspace tags`. | Lane, rule 15 |
| 2026-09-08 | Ruling 3.2's `+ Tag` action-row trigger and the popover: verified by RUNNING, not by reading | [CONFIRMED, this session] The dispatch asked whether applying a tag fails once the read is fixed. It does not. The smoke clicks an unapplied option, and the resulting `PUT /api/workspace/tags/<id>/items` carries the bearer, the stub accepts it, and the new pill appears on the detail row. There is no second defect behind the 401, and no deviation from ruling 3.2's single-trigger shape. | Lane, on measurement |
| 2026-09-08 | `Bearer ${session?.access_token}` in 21 further files: FIXED as a class, not logged as a follow-up | [CONFIRMED, census] The dispatch named five hand-rolled callers. A census against the 50 requireAuth-guarded routes found 24 files and four distinct spellings, 21 of them interpolating the token unconditionally, which sends the literal string `Bearer undefined` before the session resolves. Every one is now on the shared fetcher and the copies are deleted rather than left dormant. The admin surfaces this touches are click-driven, so most never showed the symptom; they carried it. | Rule 13, and the dispatch's "fix the CLASS" |
## Lane rsc503 (train 61, 2026-09-08): the dashboard failure state and the RSC 503s

| Date | Page / deviation | Detail | Ruled by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-08 | Dashboard (01) and the four list surfaces, the fail-soft state note gains a REASON line | [CONFIRMED] The artboards draw no failure state at all, so there is no artboard value to deviate from; this is a deviation only in that it adds a line the design package does not draw. The operator-locked sentence "Data temporarily unavailable. Refresh to retry." is unchanged. | 2026-09-08 | Shared part `ListRow`, the empty overlay anchor is CORRECT and stays | [CONFIRMED, reproduced on production today] The 2026-09-08 click-through audit read 11 row anchors with correct `aria-label`s and `innerHTML.length === 0` as a rendering defect. It is not. `a.cl-row-link` is `position:absolute; inset:0`, `gridColumn: 2 / -1` and deliberately childless: it is the stretched overlay that makes the whole row one click target (README 0.4). Measured live at 12:30 UTC, 11 of 11 row links empty with every row rendering correctly around them. Nothing changed in the component. A guard was added instead, asserting what the audit was reaching for: a row must render content and its overlay's accessible name must be the title it covers. | Lane rsc503; README 0.4, the whole row is the click target |

## Lane opsclip (train 61, production-defect lane), 2026-09-08

Source: the click-through audit of production run 2026-09-08 against train 59 (master 69b1374),
`CLICKTHROUGH-2026-09-08.md`. Every row below is a page-local divergence this lane took
deliberately while fixing a defect that was live on the site, not an artboard-fidelity choice.

| Date | Page / deviation | Detail | Ruled by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-08 | Absence, the narrow-cell rule | [CONFIRMED, measured on production] The artboard (dc.html p8) draws a bare em dash in an empty matrix cell and explains it once in the card's foot strip; ruling 2.1 fixes the absence VOCABULARY. Production obeyed the vocabulary and ignored the artboard, rendering "NOT IN PRIMARY SOURCE" into a 40px TIER track (three lines, doubling the row) and into every empty cell of a five-column matrix. The rule decided here, once, in the shared part: a narrow cell gets the dash, a wide cell gets the small-caps reason. The vocabulary is not weakened, because `variant="narrow"` still takes a reason from the closed set and still states it on `aria-label` and `title`. | Artboard for presentation, 2.1 for vocabulary |
| 2026-09-08 | Absence narrow, the em dash and NO_DATA_TOKENS | [CONFIRMED] `—` is a placeholder-name token in the app's own source-entry-filter SoT, which is why an earlier lane logged the artboard's dash as unbuildable. Two smoke specs already carve the honest-dash case out per spec. This variant needs no third allowlist: it declares itself with `data-absence`, which the guard's scan skips, so a BARE dash anywhere else still fails the guard. Narrower than an allowlist, and it cannot be borrowed by accident. | Lane; guard tightened, not relaxed |
| 2026-09-08 | Operations matrix, the headline figure a fact does not have | [CONFIRMED, columns named] Artboard 08 gives every cell a headline figure. The designed home for one is the number envelope (`value_numeric`/`unit`/`currency`, migration 267), NULL on 100% of live rows with both producers kill-switched off. Columns checked and holding no figure: `fact_label` (the description), `status`, `source_note`. There is no other numeric column on `regional_data_facts`. So the figure slot renders the envelope when present, the free-text `value` when it reads as a figure, and otherwise the Absence convention ("pending"). No figure is derived out of the prose: extracting "GBP 1.1 billion" from a sentence and presenting it as THE figure for the cell would be the build inventing a number (rule 2). | Rule 2 over the artboard's filled slot |
| 2026-09-08 | Operations matrix, expanded-row height | [CONFIRMED] The artboard's expanded Facts row is about 120px tall because each of its five cells holds one short figure and one line of description. The built row is as tall as the tallest region's real prose. That is the data, not the layout: the type, the measure and the column proportions are the artboard's. | Data-driven; logged, not fixed |
| 2026-09-08 | Operations matrix head strip, `white-space: nowrap` NOT taken | [CONFIRMED, captured and read at 1440] dc.html p8 declares `nowrap` on both halves of the head strip. The artboard's card is wider than the built page's (the audit measured the live container at 750px), and taking it clipped the meta line's last words off the card's right edge at 1440 and pushed the meta past the viewport at 375. Losing characters is the defect this lane exists to remove, so the strip wraps. | Lane; the artboard's own value would re-create the defect |
| 2026-09-08 | Column headers wrap at a WORD boundary, never `overflow-wrap: anywhere` | [CONFIRMED, captured] The first attempt gave every header cell `overflowWrap: anywhere` and rendered "IMPA / CT". Breaking inside a word loses the same legibility the truncation did, differently spelled. The IMPACT header wraps at word boundaries only; the /map register's labels keep `anywhere` because their tracks go genuinely tiny at the widths /map has no artboard for. | Lane, on measurement |
| 2026-09-08 | Section index tabs bounded at 150px | [CONFIRMED] The artboard uses SHORT tab labels ("S1 Cost baseline"); the product passes each section's full title ("S1 Operational cost baseline"), which is why seven of them overflowed and cut mid-glyph. Shortening the product's section titles would change page content, so the TAB is bounded instead and truncates with a real ellipsis, full label on `title`. 150px is measured against the artboard's own longest tab. | Artboard's treatment, product's content |
| 2026-09-08 | /settings, "See audit log" removed rather than wired | [CONFIRMED: no such route under src/app, no audit-log table, the only `audit` identifiers in src/ are admin-side verification helpers] Artboard 15 draws the link. There is nothing to link it to, and production shipped it as the product's only `#`-href anchor, dead on click. Per ruling 1.1's class it is removed and the notice text stands alone; `Masthead` now refuses to render a notice link without a target, so it cannot come back as a decoration. | Ruling 1.1 over the artboard |
| 2026-09-08 | Dashboard, "All N changes" target: a new `?sort=` contract | [CONFIRMED] The artboard draws this as a link but names no target. "The changes" is the regulations list ordered newest-first, and sort had no URL contract (local `useState` in all four ledgers). `SORT_FACET_PARAM`/`sortFromSearchParam` sit beside `BAND_FACET_PARAM` in the same one home. A link to an unordered list would have satisfied the letter of "not dead" and none of its point. | Lane; ruling 1.1's class |
| 2026-09-08 | CardFoot link padding grows the footer a few px | [CONFIRMED] Carried forward from lane comp-11's own row: law 2's 24px floor is met with real padding because the surrounding Card clips overflow and would clip a negative-margin hit area. Now applies to BOTH sides of the strip rather than one call site. | Law 2 |
| 2026-09-08 | Guard, the squeezed-title detector measures a table title against its CELL | [CONFIRMED] Making the matrix fit its card meant a long dimension name wraps inside its own column instead of widening the table. Measured against the whole card, every such name reads as "squeezed to 12% of its card" whether the table is laid out well or badly. A title inside a `td`/`th` is budgeted by its column, so that is what it is now measured against. This is a detector correction, not a relaxation: the same title still fails when it does not fit the column it was given. | Lane, rule 15 |

## LANE COUNTS (train 61, lane/counts-2026-09-08), 2026-09-08

Production-defect lane: numbers that contradict each other on the same screen, from the
2026-09-08 click-through audit of carosledge.com (train 59, master 69b1374). These are not
artboard-fidelity rows. Each one is a place where the product said two different things about one
figure, or said something the data does not support; the deviations below are what the FIX had to
decide, not what it chose to leave undone.

| Date | Page / deviation | Detail | Ruled by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-08 | /map, /regulations, /market, /research, /operations: the masthead figure and the body figure are now ONE function each | [CONFIRMED, this lane] The audit found three jurisdiction counts on /map and three Monitor counts across the dashboard and /regulations. In every case two surfaces computed the same figure from two different derivations. The deviation from a pure display fix, stated: nothing was patched at the display. `src/lib/map/jurisdiction-rollup.ts` is now the one jurisdiction derivation, `getPublicSurfaceCounts("regulations")` the one band-tile source for both screens that draw those tiles, and `liveFacetCounts` the one facet/total derivation for the four ledgers. | Lane, on the operator's standing rule that the product's value is that its numbers are right |
| 2026-09-08 | /regulations and its three siblings: band tiles and the surface total CHANGE under a filter, where the artboard shows them at rest | [CONFIRMED, this lane] The artboard draws one state, unfiltered, and its Filters card carries the caption "Counts are live for the current selection." The build satisfied the picture and contradicted the caption. The caption is the artboard's own text, so the counts move: at rest they are the corpus RPC figures the artboard's numbers came from, and under any facet they are tallies over the current selection. A reader who applies a filter therefore sees numbers the artboard does not draw, deliberately. | The artboard's own caption |
| 2026-09-08 | List surfaces, facet state is written to the URL; the artboards draw no URL | [CONFIRMED, this lane] `?band=` already existed (audit item 1.1, train 59) and no other facet wrote anything, so a filtered view could not be linked or reloaded. Every facet now round-trips through the URL under one contract (`useListSurfaceFilter`). The artboards say nothing either way about URL state; this extends an existing in-product contract rather than inventing a control, and adds no visible element. | Lane, extending BAND_FACET_PARAM's own precedent |
| 2026-09-08 | /research now renders per request, where it was statically prerendered | [CONFIRMED, this lane, by reading the build output] Once the ledger reads facet state from the URL, a statically prerendered page cannot server-render it: Next defers the whole `useSearchParams` boundary to the client, and the prerendered `.next/server/app/research.html` came out as 28KB of frame with none of the rows. Its three sibling list surfaces already render per request. The route joins them. What is given up is the cached HTML, not the cached queries: all three data reads stay `unstable_cache`-wrapped with their own TTL and tag. | Lane, on measurement |
| 2026-09-08 | /admin, the provisional queue's badge population changed in the DATABASE, not the display | [CONFIRMED, live DB] 489 pending_review + 2 needs_more_data = 491, and the table rendering all 491 was right. Migration 314 redefines `admin_attention_counts` to count both statuses, and was APPLIED to production ahead of this code commit per the two-track policy, then verified (`provisional_sources_pending` = 491). The status vocabulary now has one home, `src/lib/admin/provisional-review-queue.ts`, which the app-side read imports and which the migration's own SQL comment cites. | Two-track migration policy; the queue as rendered is the population |
| 2026-09-08 | Nav badge "Community": the field changed meaning, and the membership figure was kept | [CONFIRMED, this lane] `CommunitySurfaceCounts.activeGroups` counted groups the workspace's members had JOINED, under two labels that say rooms ("Community 1" in the rail, "1 regional rooms" on the dashboard) against a page reading "7 regional rooms". The field splits into `regionalRooms` (the ROOMS roster the /community page itself renders) and `joinedGroups` (the membership tally, which the /community page states in its own "you are in N" sentence). No figure is lost; each now sits under the label that describes it. | Lane; the page's own header is the authority for what "regional rooms" counts |
| 2026-09-08 | /watchlist, "SINCE YOUR LAST VISIT" is REMOVED as a claim, not merely deduplicated | [CONFIRMED, this lane] The audit's finding was that the phrase printed twice and never gave a date. The stronger finding underneath it: the phrase was false. `GET /api/notices` applies a fixed 30-day window (its own `DEFAULT_WINDOW_DAYS`) and no caller sends `?since=`; the product records no last visit. The route has always returned the `since` it applied and the hook discarded it. The card now states that window once, with its real start date, and the two shared components' default empty copy stops making the claim everywhere else they are mounted. | Rule 2, never fabricate |
| 2026-09-08 | /watchlist, the empty-state and foot copy point at the list pages, not at this page | [CONFIRMED, this lane, and the audit's own claim is now STALE against this base] The audit (train 59) said the copy "references a control that does not exist on that page". On train 60 it does exist: the compose-11 spec measures a 44px overflow control on every watchlist row and it passes. So the literal defect is already closed by the base. The copy was still ambiguous — "any row's ⋯ menu" reads as the menu on the row in front of you, which is an unwatch menu, not a way to find something new to watch — so it now names where the watch menu is. Recorded as a partial [REFUTED] rather than claimed as a fix. | Base already fixed the literal claim; wording changed on its own merits |
| 2026-09-08 | Detail pages, the FACT label is stripped at RENDER, and the pipeline output is untouched | [CONFIRMED against the live corpus] Stored `content_md` carries the label in three shapes (`FACT: "…"`, `**FACT:** "…"`, `**<lead-in> — FACT:** "…"`) and wraps the claim in straight quotes; `FactCard` adds its own typographic quotes. The parser now strips the label and one layer of surrounding quotes. This follows `fact-paragraphs.ts`'s own standing rule (parse at render time, never change what the pipeline emits) rather than a data migration, so no published row is touched. | The file's own render-time rule |
| 2026-09-08 | Detail sub-lines, a repeated segment is DROPPED rather than the producers being rewired | [CONFIRMED, this lane] /market/[id] printed its source name twice because the page uses `publisher` for both the breadcrumb and the deck's first part. `joinMetaSegments` drops a part already present in the line, first occurrence winning. The alternative, making each page's two producers aware of each other, would have to be redone for each of the four detail types; this is one function all four now compose through. The regulation sub-line had the same latent repetition (jurisdiction and publisher, both twice) and is fixed by the same call. | Lane, fixing the class |
| 2026-09-08 | /admin, "DISCOVERED 3 mo ago / 4 mo ago" is CORRECT and is NOT changed | [CONFIRMED, live DB] The audit contrasted it with the artboard's "2-6 days ago". The 491 queued rows genuinely carry `created_at` between 2026-04-05 and 2026-06-01, so the relative-time rendering is right and the artboard's figure is mock data. The queue is months stale because build mode holds the scrape cadence off (CLAUDE.md rule 16), which is the expected consequence of a standing operator decision, not a defect. Logged as data, changed nowhere. | Rule 16; data, not rendering |
| 2026-09-08 | /admin, the newest-join line renders "(no profile)" where a member has no name | [CONFIRMED, this lane] It printed a raw UUID fragment. `MembersPanel`'s display chain, whose own header reads "NO raw UUIDs render in member rows", ended in a `user_id.slice(0, 8)` fallback, and `WorkspacesUsageRow` did not use that chain at all. The chain moved to `src/lib/admin/member-display-name.ts` and the slice is GONE from it, so a member with no name, display name or email renders the absence rather than an identifier the reader cannot act on. The artboard draws a UUID fragment here; it is not followed. | Rule 2 over the artboard, which the audit itself records as an artboard defect |

## lane mobile60 (2026-09-08): the mobile 390 audit specs

Eight audit specs now measure the product at viewport 390 against the MOBILE 390 SPEC text
(`fsi-app/.discipline/rendering/audit/spec/mobile-*.json`). No 390 artboard PNG has reached the
repo; the text spec is the binding source until one does, and when one arrives the image wins over
the text and every difference below is re-decided against it.

| Date | Deviation | Evidence | Reason | Who ruled |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-08 | The drawer footer is ONE row, not the text spec's "two unlabelled 44px rows: Account with the workspace name, Admin with the OWNER badge" | [CONFIRMED, measured this session, `spec/mobile-18-drawer.json`] The drawer renders one 44px row, the logged-in person's name with the workspace name right-aligned, opening the menu that holds Workspace profile, Admin panel, Settings and Sign out. | The operator's 2026-09-07 third-set ruling ("we dont need seperate account and admin buttons visable if they pop up as options when you clicked on the logged in person name there", "signout lives in account") is LATER than the mobile 390 text spec and explicitly covers the footer. The later ruling wins; the text spec's DRAWER footer wording is stale exactly as the settings mobile note was. | operator, 2026-09-07 (third set) |
| 2026-09-08 | The rail's FILTERS card is not drawn below 768 on the four list surfaces that have a mobile chip strip | [CONFIRMED, measured this session] Before this lane the folded rail's FILTERS card AND the mobile chip strip AND the Filters sheet button all rendered at 390: two live controls over one set of `facetGroups`. | The mobile 390 spec designs exactly one filter surface at this width ("chip groups scroll sideways as whole units"; "facet counts and the workspace-tag facet open in a sheet from Filters"), and CLAUDE.md rule 13 forbids the second. The rest of the rail still folds under the content, as the spec's BREAKPOINTS section says. | coordinator default (operator: "why are you showing me this and not just fixing it") |
| 2026-09-08 | /watchlist KEEPS its rail FILTERS card at 390 | [CONFIRMED, measured this session, `spec/mobile-11-watchlist.json`] WatchlistSurface renders no chip strip and no Filters sheet: its facets live only in that rail card. | Hiding it would leave the page with no filter surface at all at 390, a worse defect than the duplication the rule above removes. The hide rule is gated on a class `ListSurfaceShell` adds only when it is actually rendering the chip strip. | coordinator default |
| 2026-09-08 | The list row's 44x44 overflow control is a fixed gutter at the row's right edge, not an item of the wrapping line-2 flow | [CONFIRMED, measured at 390 and read off `built/mobile-01-dashboard.png`] The metadata ahead of it (impact 72-108px, date+days 130-142px, tier 30px, three 9px gaps) already fills the 296px content width, so with `margin-left: auto` the control wrapped onto a line of its own in EVERY row and rendered as a 44px box with a dangling left rule and nothing beside it. Rows measured 122-140px tall; they are now 87. | The spec writes "the overflow control pushed right: 44x44, ..., border-left". A control alone under the metadata with a border-left is the orphan/collision class the operator's visual-pass standard forbids. The row grid is still the spec's literal `3px 1fr`; the gutter is padding on the row, not a third track. | coordinator default |
| 2026-09-08 | The desktop column header row is not drawn below 768 | [CONFIRMED, measured at 390] Its eight fixed tracks need 489px before the title column gets a pixel; at 390 it ran ~160px past the card with its IMPACT / DUE / TIMELINE / TIER labels over the page edge. | The mobile 390 spec has no column header at all (the row is two-line and the timeline column is dropped), so it is not reflowed, it is not shown. | coordinator default |
| 2026-09-08 | `SectionHeading` stacks below 768 and `CardFoot` wraps | [CONFIRMED, measured at 390] Both are `white-space: nowrap`, sized for the 778px content column; at 390 the dashboard's "DUE NEXT · 5 ITEMS / SORTED BY NEXT DATE" head ran 286px past the card and the card foot's legend 5px past it. | The mobile 390 spec states no measure for either; the reflow is the same one `globals.css`'s `.cl-section-head` already applies to the detail surfaces' heads. Nothing else about them changes. | coordinator default |
| 2026-09-08 | The vertical timeline's date gutter is left WRAPPING, not held on one line | [CONFIRMED, measured at 390] An ISO milestone date ("2027-01-01") at 11px needs ~57px in Plus Jakarta Sans and ~70px in the fallback face a host without that font uses, against the spec's fixed 62px gutter. `white-space: nowrap` was tried and, in the wide fallback, the date overflowed its own box to the RIGHT and lay under the dot column (`text-align: right` does not pull overflowing content back). | A date that wraps at its hyphen in one face is worse-looking; a date lying across the dots is wrong in every face. The 390 artboard, when it lands, states what date FORM the design intends in a 62px gutter. | coordinator default |
| 2026-09-08 | ~~An unscored row at 390 can show three absence reasons across two wrapped lines~~ **SUPERSEDED / REFUTED 2026-09-08 by lane mobfix61 — see that lane's section below. The reasoning below is wrong on both halves: the operator photographed exactly this on production the next morning (D-M4), and ruling 2.1 removes the literal "UNSCORED" everywhere rather than permitting it as one of three. It is a layout AND a product defect, it renders identically at 1440, and it is now fixed in the shared parts for both widths. Corrected in place, not deleted (CLAUDE.md rule 13 corollary).** | [CONFIRMED, read off `built/mobile-01-dashboard.png`: "unscored" (impact), "pending" (due), "not in primary source" (tier)] | Data-driven, not a layout defect: each cell states its own absence, which is the product's Absence convention at every width, and the desktop lays the same three out in three columns. The spec's "never a second row" is about the unscored REASON, which is on the baseline's own line. Recorded, not changed. | logged, no change |
| 2026-09-08 | The 375 exemption list: the `map` entry is inactive and suppressing nothing | [CONFIRMED, measured this session] `latestTrainWave()` reads 58, so `activeExemptions()` returns empty, and the rendering guard was run with it inactive and PASSED. The five list pages' entries were already removed (FOLD-56 + lane moblist). | The operator's ruling ("exemption confirmed, expires wave 58") has run its course by its own terms. The entry is kept as the record of that ruling and of the wave it expired at; deleting it would erase both, and the mechanism around it is live for the next page that needs one. | operator, 2026-09-07 (second set, item 2) |
| 2026-09-08 | The list-surface mobile specs measure the page component, not the AppShell frame | [CONFIRMED] The `compose-02/04/06/08/11` mounts render the ledger component alone. | The TOP BAR and DRAWER regions are measured once, by `mobile-01-dashboard.json` and `mobile-18-drawer.json`, rather than repeated on five surfaces that cannot see them. The list specs assert `.cl-facets-desktop` and `.cl-list-row-header` as forbids instead of as hidden elements, because those surfaces render neither at any width and a `display: none` expectation would report NOT BUILT rather than the truth. | coordinator default |

## lane mobfix61 (2026-09-08): the operator's mobile report, measured and closed

The operator photographed production (train 58, `3230c7d2`) on his phone on 2026-09-08 and wrote:
"the mobile pages are completly misspaced. search bar touched bounding box, words go off page.
awareness text is in wrong place and numbers inside box hit outside. all text spacing is off. map
page complete overlaps sections." Five screenshots. Each was measured against a fresh 390 capture of
this lane's base rather than against the sibling lane's report; three of his defects were already
closed on the base, four were not.

| Date | Deviation | Evidence | Reason | Who ruled |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-08 | The masthead body's mobile bottom padding is **16px**, not the mobile 390 spec's literal "padding 14px 16px 0" | [CONFIRMED, measured at 390 on `compose-02-regulations`] Before: masthead card bottom 186.3, body bottom 185.3, command bar bottom 185.3 — the bar's bottom edge sat ON the card's own 1px bottom border, with zero padding beneath it, on every mobile surface. After: card bottom 202.3, bar bottom 185.3, 17px of clearance (16px padding + the 1px border). | The spec's trailing "0" describes where the body's CONTENT STARTS, not the card's padding below its last child, and the artboard draws padding there. A card whose content touches its own border is the contained-by-its-parent rule broken whatever the spec text reads. 16px is the same spec's own bottom measure for the tile/content container directly beneath, so the two agree. This is the operator's "search bar touched bounding box". | coordinator default, on the operator's report (D-M5) |
| 2026-09-08 | A list row renders **exactly one** absence reason, and the literal "unscored" is never that reason | [CONFIRMED at BOTH widths, by attack] The impact, due and tier cells each rendered their own `<Absence>`, so a row missing all three drew a dashed baseline, then UNSCORED, then PENDING, then NOT IN PRIMARY SOURCE on a line of its own. The audit's own eight "no literal UNSCORED" forbids all reported MATCH while this shipped — they matched `textContent` ("unscored") against an uppercase pattern the CSS produces, so they had been vacuous since they were written. With the harness fixed, `compose-04/06/08` went red at **1440** and `mobile-01/03/04/06/08` at 390: not a mobile defect at all. | Ruling 2.1 (P1, operator, 2026-09-07) and the mobile 390 spec's LIST ROW section both say one reason, never a second row. The reason is now chosen ONCE by `ABSENCE_PRECEDENCE` in `Absence.tsx` and rendered in the cell that owns that dimension; `ListRow`'s `reasonSlot` makes "at most one" structural rather than a convention. | operator ruling 2.1, applied (D-M4) |
| 2026-09-08 | `"unscored"` is in the `AbsenceReason` vocabulary but is never chosen as a row's reason | [CONFIRMED] It names the STATE the 30px dashed baseline already draws, so rendering it as a token as well is a tautology — and it is the literal ruling 2.1 removes by name. Precedence is `connect data` > `not in primary source` > `pending`; a row whose only missing dimension is its score reads `pending`. | The vocabulary is the operator's and is left closed and intact; what changed is which member the row anatomy may select. Written down in `Absence.tsx`'s own header so the rule lives beside the code that applies it, per this lane's brief ("if there is no precedence ... the rule is written down"). | coordinator default (D-M4) |
| 2026-09-08 | The map page's outer grid folds to one column below 1280 and takes the mobile page padding below 768 | [CONFIRMED root cause, twice over] `MapPageView.tsx`'s only media query named `.cl-map-grid`; the two-column grid is `.cl-map-outer`, and `.cl-map-grid` is the inner content column, a `display:flex` element on which `grid-template-columns` is inert. The 300px rail track therefore survived at every width and at 390 the rail cards were laid out over the MODE / BAND / REGION chip rows — the operator's "map page complete overlaps sections", reproduced exactly on this lane's base before the fix. | The same one-track rule every other page shell already uses (`ListSurfaceShell`'s `.cl-list-surface-grid`), with `minmax(0, 1fr)` rather than a bare `1fr`. The dead `.cl-map-grid` rule is deleted rather than left dormant (rule 13). | coordinator default (D-M3) |
| 2026-09-08 | The labelled sort/window option set is one non-wrapping sideways-scrolling line below 768 | [CONFIRMED, read off this lane's own 390 capture of /regulations] `ListSurfaceSortRow` had no mobile treatment at all, so its `flexWrap: wrap` broke the segmented control across two lines and stranded the last option ("My order") on a line of its own, away from the SORT label that gives it meaning. | The mobile 390 spec's FILTERS rule applies to every labelled option set on the page: "chip groups scroll sideways as whole units so a group never loses its label". Same mechanism `.cl-facets-mobile` already uses. Part of the operator's "all text spacing is off". | coordinator default (D-M6) |
| 2026-09-08 | The band-tile ROW is a shared part (`BandTileRow`), not markup repeated at each call site | [CONFIRMED] The grid was hand-written at three call sites; the class carrying the 2x2 mobile rule was on two of them, so the onboarding preview still rendered four 67px tiles across at 390 with labels clipped to IMMEDIAT / AWARENE after the other two were fixed. | CLAUDE.md rule 13. A container that must be REMEMBERED at every call site is the defect; there is now one container and nothing to remember. The desktop DOM is byte-identical for the dashboard and the list shell, so no 1440 spec is re-baselined. | coordinator default (D-M1) |
| 2026-09-08 | The design audit's `textMatch` compares against RENDERED text, not source text | [CONFIRMED by attack] Nineteen spec files carried a `textMatch: "UNSCORED"` / `"NOT SCORED"` forbid; the product writes those words lower case and uppercases them in CSS, so `textContent` returned "unscored" and every one of those forbids matched nothing, at every viewport, for as long as they existed. They reported MATCH while the operator was photographing the literal on his phone. | CLAUDE.md rule 15: a guard is proven by attack, not by presence. The fix belongs in the harness rather than in nineteen individually weakened specs. Matching is now the UNION of source and rendered text so an ordinary target (authored against the source, to narrow a selector) is unaffected — matching rendered-only sent 35 targets NOT BUILT. | coordinator default |
| 2026-09-08 | `.cl-facets-desktop`'s `display: none` rule is deleted from `ListSurfaceShell` | [CONFIRMED, found by F40 on its first run] No element in the app carries that class; the specs already assert it as a forbid. The strip was retired when the filters moved to the rail and the rule outlived it. | Rule 13: dormant code is removed, not left. Its own npmtest already asserts the element is not mounted, so nothing was weakened to allow this. | coordinator default |

## FOLD-61 (train/wave61-2026-09-08, the six-lane fold), 2026-09-08

The four lanes cut from wave60 (tags401, rsc503, opsclip, counts) and the two cut from wave59
(mobile60, mobfix61) each carry their own section above. These are the rows the FOLD decided,
plus the defects it found that no lane could see from inside one worktree.

| Date | Page / deviation | Detail | Ruled by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-08 | The F40 collision: two fitness functions, one number | [CONFIRMED] Lane TAGS-401 and lane mobfix61 each added a fitness function numbered F40 with a different name (`authed-api-fetch`, `dead-media-query-class`) and each claimed invariant RD-65. Both are good and both stay. mobfix61's is renumbered F41 / RD-66 / skill Section 4 category 41, because TAGS-401 landed first in this train and its number was already cited by four discipline files, a smoke spec and a skill section, so renumbering it would have touched strictly more citations. The skill's normative-marker baseline is 50, not the 49 each lane wrote: each counted its own single new marker and the fold carries both. Both selftests were re-run red-then-green after the renumbering, so it is proven rather than assumed. | Fold, rule 15 |
| 2026-09-08 | ListRow's TIER cell: opsclip's dash and mobfix61's one-token rule are ONE mechanism | [CONFIRMED, artboard-ruled] The two lanes appeared to disagree about what an absent value shows. They do not: "narrow" is a property of the CELL, not of the page. mobfix61's `ABSENCE_PRECEDENCE`/`reasonSlot` decides WHICH reason a row shows and which cell owns it; opsclip's `variant="narrow"` decides how that one reason is DRAWN where the cell cannot hold the phrase. They meet in the tier cell, the only 40px fixed track of the three, and the artboard decides: dc.html p2 and p8 draw a dash there and explain it once in the card foot, so the tier slot draws the dash and the impact and due slots, which have room, draw the words. | Fold; dc.html p2/p8 |
| 2026-09-08 | ...and at 390 that same cell shows the WORDS, because the operator's own spec says so | [CONFIRMED, found by eye at 390] Below 768 the row reflows to `3px 1fr` and the tier cell is no longer narrow, and the mobile 390 spec's operator prose is explicit: "Absence keeps its small-caps reason". So the ONE token takes the form its cell can hold, the dash above 768 and the reason below it, in ONE element: two sibling spans would be simpler to read but the design audit counts ELEMENTS (`count: 1` on `.cl-list-row .cl-absence`, mobfix61's D-M4 guarantee), so the swap is done with `content` on a pseudo-element of that single span. `aria-label` and `title` carry the closed-vocabulary reason at both widths, so nothing about ruling 2.1's vocabulary depends on the viewport. | Fold; the mobile 390 spec's own prose |
| 2026-09-08 | Artboard 10's jurisdiction register: the NAME column computed to 18.5px at 390 | [CONFIRMED, measured] The fold's first real product defect. The register's six tracks (`3px 1fr 1fr 110px 80px 40px`, five 14px gaps) need 303px of fixed width and gap; the row is 356px at 390, leaving 37px for BOTH flexible tracks, so the jurisdiction name rendered as roughly one character and an ellipsis. Neither lane could see it: map60 built the variant when no 390 spec existed, and mobfix61's mobile-10-map spec was written against the shared `.cl-list-row` that variant replaced, so its four register rows reported NOT BUILT against the row that shipped. No mobile artboard for the register exists and none is invented; what governs is the operator's "the frame collapses; every part is the desktop part at a smaller measure", so every part still renders in the same order and type and only the measure changes: name over meta in one flexible column, band over count in a narrow one, the 40px arrow full height. | Fold, rule 13; the mobile 390 spec's own prose |
| 2026-09-08 | ...and the band column is 84px, a number, not `max-content` | [CONFIRMED, measured] The first value tried, 68px, clipped IMMEDIATE to "IMMEDIAT" with no ellipsis, which is lane opsclip's own defect class in a cell whose text is fixed, short and known at build time. Measured max-content for the widest label present is 79.5px; AWARENESS is the same nine characters in wider glyphs, so 84px clears the whole four-word band vocabulary. `max-content` would fit too, and is rejected deliberately: it would make the resolved track list depend on WHICH bands the data happens to contain, which is not something an exact-equality spec row should be measuring. | Fold |
| 2026-09-08 | ...and the reflow's own regression, caught by the guard within minutes | [CONFIRMED, then REFUTED as shipped] The register's stretched click overlay is an absolutely positioned GRID ITEM, so its containing block is the grid AREA it is placed in and its `grid-row: 1 / -1` counts lines in the EXPLICIT grid. With the new rows implicit, `-1` resolved to line 2 and the overlay measured 24px inside a 51px row: four targets under the law-2 44px floor, which the rendering guard reported at 375. The rows are declared now. Recorded because the mechanism is not obvious and the next lane adding a mobile grid to a row with an overlay will hit it. | Fold, rule 15 |
| 2026-09-08 | /watchlist's changed-since strip read "changed in since aug 8, 2026" | [CONFIRMED, found by eye at 1440] Lane counts correctly replaced the card's doubled "SINCE YOUR LAST VISIT" with the window's real start date, and built ONE label, "Since Aug 8, 2026", for the card head. The strip beside it reused that label lower-cased, so it rendered "in since" and a lower-cased month under a head that uppercases the same date. The card head and the strip are different SENTENCES and cannot share a phrase: the DATE is derived once now and presented twice, as a head label and as a clause. No spec measured the strip's prose; two rows do now, one forbid and one positive, both proven by attack. | Fold, visual pass |
| 2026-09-08 | /admin: one screen, two numbers for one queue, at a third site | [CONFIRMED, found by eye at 1440] Lane counts fixed the admin badge by making `admin_attention_counts()` count the queue AS RENDERED (migration 314, 491 = 489 pending_review + 2 needs_more_data) and pointing the tab badge at it. The provisional card's own head still read `rows.length`, the loaded PAGE, so the screen showed "Provisional review · 489" in the tab and "4 pending" directly under it, and would show a page cap against 489 in production. Both numbers come from the same queue now, and when the card holds fewer rows than the queue has it SAYS so ("N pending · showing M") rather than renaming the queue after its own page. | Fold; lane counts' own rule, applied to the site it did not reach |
| 2026-09-08 | The shared RowTable had no treatment below 768 at all | [CONFIRMED, measured at 390] Found by the fold's own overflow sweep, not by any spec: RowTable's callers are the three surfaces the mobile 390 work never reached, /admin's ORGANIZATIONS and SOURCES tables, /account's member list, /community's discussion feed. Every column kept its designed px track at 390, so the row was wider than the card and the card's `overflow: hidden` cut the cells: header labels lost characters mid-word with NO ellipsis (Discussion 77px past its cell, Source 50px, Member 53px, Name 36px) and so did row sub-lines (plasticsnews.com 97px, emsa.europa.eu 89px). A column header is the `mustFit` class lane opsclip established. The table scrolls sideways below 768 as one unit, on the same declared-strip mechanism (`data-guard-strip`) the chip groups, theme strip, obligations strip, community tabs and detail chip row already use, so every column keeps its artboard width and the reader reaches all of them. Desktop rendering is unchanged. | Fold, rule 13; the mobile 390 spec's own prose |
| 2026-09-08 | ...and the strip's PLACEMENT in the DOM is load-bearing | [CONFIRMED] The first attempt wrapped the table in a new element, which deepened `[data-audit="orgs-table"] > div > div:first-child` and reported NOT BUILT on two composition specs against a header that renders correctly. The second put the `<style>` element first inside the existing root, which made `div:first-child` match nothing for the same reason. The class and attribute go on the existing root and the rule sits at the END of it. Recorded because both failures looked like product regressions and were neither. | Fold |
| 2026-09-08 | /settings, the sector tile's longest label lost 6px at 390 | [CONFIRMED, measured] `minWidth: 0` lets the cell shrink and the label wraps between words, but "Pharmaceutical" is 6px wider than the tile at 390, and a word that cannot fit its box is clipped rather than wrapped. `overflowWrap: break-word` breaks only a word that does not otherwise fit, so every shorter label and every wider viewport is unchanged. | Fold, rule 13 |
| 2026-09-08 | The sort row's sideways scroller was never declared to the guard | [CONFIRMED, by the guard] mobfix61's D-M6 fix makes the sort options scroll sideways below 768, correctly, but did not mark the strip, so the rendering guard measured "My order" at right=410 on a 375 viewport with no strip to carry it and reported three UX-smoke failures on /regulations. One attribute, the convention five other strips already carry. The fix was right; only its disclosure to the guard was missing. | Fold |
| 2026-09-08 | The overflow sweep is a script now, not a fold ritual | [CONFIRMED] Trains 59 and 60 each ran a horizontal-overflow/clipped-text/orphan sweep by hand at the fold and neither left anything behind, so train 61 wrote it a third time. `.discipline/rendering/audit/overflow-sweep.mjs`, wired as `npm run audit:overflow` (F25 execution-wiring), sweeps every mount at any width. Its clipped-text detector is HORIZONTAL-only past a 3px floor, calibrated against its own first run: 30-odd hits were all 2-3px VERTICAL overshoot on Anton display numerals, where the glyph box exceeds its computed line box and nothing is lost. A run that loses characters loses them horizontally. Mounts that pin their own viewport are component fixtures, not pages, and are reported as pinned rather than counted. | Fold, rule 15 (a proof that does not execute is not a proof) |
| 2026-09-08 | Migration 314 was on disk and unlisted | [CONFIRMED, by the C3 consistency check] The migration file lane counts carries in its tree had no row in `docs/inventories/migrations.md`, which failed `override-check`. The row is added and states plainly that the migration is ALREADY APPLIED TO PRODUCTION AND VERIFIED BY THE COORDINATOR and must not be re-applied, so a later session reading the inventory cannot mistake it for pending DDL. | Fold; CLAUDE.md standing rule 3 |
| 2026-09-08 | No file was reported BINARY, checked rather than assumed | [CONFIRMED, mechanical] Train 59's literal-NUL class was checked first: zero NUL bytes under `src/` and `.discipline/`, and zero source files git treats as binary anywhere in this fold's diff against master. The escape-sequence fix has now held for two trains. | Fold |

## LANE MAPCLIP (2026-09-08): the declared clipping viewport

| Date | Page / deviation | Detail | Ruled by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-08 | The map canvas is DECLARED a clipping viewport (`data-guard-clip`), the one carve-out in the clipped-overflow rule | [CONFIRMED locally, CI half unproven here] Train 61's PR #610 was green on nine checks and red on the rendering guard in CI only: `map-page:populated@1280`, one `img[leaflet-tile leaflet-tile-loaded]` at right=1408px. Leaflet lays a tile grid deliberately wider than its frame and pans it inside that frame's own overflow, so `getBoundingClientRect` reports a tile's UNCLIPPED rect and a tile at the frame's edge reads as past the page edge while nothing the reader must read is cut off. A tile is rendering substrate, not a text run. Declared in the product (`MapPageView.tsx`, the `data-testid="map-canvas"` frame, which also states `overflow: hidden` so the declaration is honest) and in the smoke spec's stand-in for it, and read by `measureUx` exactly as `data-guard-strip` is read. Narrow twice over: the ancestor must ACTUALLY clip, and it must ITSELF sit inside the viewport, so a declaring element that overflows carries nothing and is reported like any other box. Proven by attack at all twelve viewports (fixtures `map-clip-declared`, `map-clip-undeclared-PREFIX`, `map-clip-self-overflow-PREFIX`); `overflow: hidden` on the map canvas is NOT the fix and was refused, since swallowing overflow is the defect class this detector exists to catch. | Coordinator diagnosis, verified this lane; CLAUDE.md rule 15 |

## LANE LAYOUTGUARD (2026-09-08): the site-wide layout guard, L1 to L12

The operator's SITE-WIDE LAYOUT GUARD dispatch (2026-09-08) turned twelve of the defects he has been
finding by eye into twelve mechanical rules. This lane built them into the EXISTING rendering guard
rather than a thirteenth harness, ran them over all 17 routes at 1440 and 1024, fixed the shared
parts that are unambiguously shared, and routed the rest. The full failure table, by rule and by
route, with the owning part named for every group, is `docs/audits/layout-guard-2026-09-08.md`.

RULE PROVENANCE. L3 and L11 were ALREADY COVERED (audit/overflow-sweep.mjs and ux-assert.mjs's
detectClippedOverflow / detectClippedText); L11 is called, never restated. L2 and L9 are EXTENDED
(assertions.mjs's detectBoundsViolations did row-versus-cell containment only; ux-assert's
detectSmallTargets is law 2's mobile floor and stays as it is, because the operator's L9 is a
different, site-wide floor). L1, L4, L5, L6, L7, L8, L10 and L12 are NEW.

DECISIONS TAKEN WHERE THE OPERATOR'S TEXT NEEDED ONE, all argued in the code they govern:

L1 is a COMPUTED-STYLE test on the rendered tree, not a source grep, because a page can satisfy a
grep and still defeat the frame with a nested wrapper. It reads each route's frame against the
ARTBOARD's own numbers (from the dc.html), never against the app's source. Below 1280 it expects the
STACKED form, which is README 0.3 verbatim, so ruling R10 (no invented 1024 behaviour) is untouched.

L2's exclusion set is exactly four cases (parent/child; an element the L5 allowlist names; an
overlap under 4px on either axis; an invisible box) and is proven not to swallow a real overlap: the
test feeds it the operator's own /admin defect shape and requires it to fire.

L5 excuses a CONTAINED absolute decoration (a positioned ancestor whose box holds it entirely, no
text of its own), because a contained decoration cannot collide with anything outside it. `fixed`
and `sticky` are never excused that way.

L7's allowlist is declared by the shared components that draw display type, so a page must reach for
one of them. A seventh entry beyond the operator's six is declared: the nav card wordmark, which
every one of the 17 artboards draws in Anton.

L8 compares RENDERED text (the shared browser/rendered-text.mjs, the one implementation the design
audit also uses). "PENDING" must be the whole of its own run; the first run matched it inside "4
inputs pending" and "489 pending, showing 4, approved", which are sentences, not absence tokens. The
other three tokens are matched anywhere.

L10's manifests are GENERATED FROM THE ARTBOARDS (generate-manifests.mjs reads the dc.html), never
from the build, and card identity truncates at the first middot so a live count does not make every
card on every route report as outside the artboard.

ONE CORRECTION TO THE OPERATOR'S FRAMING, stated once and not argued. L10 says the manifest
"replaces the deviation log that was never written to". This log HAS been written to throughout: it
carries rulings R1 to R10 and a dated section for every UI lane in trains 59, 60 and 61. What it
cannot do is fail a build, which is exactly what the manifest adds. The manifest therefore READS
this log (manifests.mjs deviationsFromLog) rather than replacing it, so the two cannot drift into
two vocabularies.

| Date | Page | Deviation | Reason | Who ruled |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-08 | all | The 783 findings the first run left are held by a DATED baseline that expires at wave 65 | Every one belongs to a part another lane is actively rewriting; a new finding fails the build from today. Not a relaxation: the mechanism, the expiry and the argument are in layout-guard/baseline.mjs | lane layoutguard, following the operator's "no train lands with a failure" and the exemptions-375 precedent |
| 2026-09-08 | /settings | Frame padding stays `18px 40px 0` against artboard p15's `18px 40px 40px` | The page splits its frame into two stacked regions and the second carries the bottom padding, so the right value is a composition question for the settings lane, not a one-number edit | lane layoutguard, routed not fixed |
| 2026-09-08 | all | `.cl-row-card` (RecalculationNotice) carries card chrome and fails L6 for having no top rule | A row wearing a card's chrome is a card by the artboard's own definition. Reported, routed to the figures lane, not silenced | lane layoutguard |

## lane briefdata (train 61, 2026-09-08): the two brief cards' data path, and the watch write

Operator ruling, verbatim: "do next and whats changed need to stay populated", plus a mid-flight
addition, "watchlist also not populated". This lane owns the DATA PATH only; the sibling lane
listrow is rewriting the dashboard's row RENDERING at the same time, so nothing here restyles a card
or touches `ListRow`.

**Threaded props (declared, because they touch `DashboardBrief.tsx`).** Two, both server-computed:

- `dueNextWindow`, the Due-next card's aside. It was built inside the client component from
  `nowIso`; it now states which window the SELECTED ROWS span, so it is built where the rows are
  selected (`dueNextWindowLabel`, src/lib/dashboard/brief-rows.ts). The component's `weekOfLabel`
  `useMemo` and its `formatLocaleDate`/`nowFrom` imports are deleted with it rather than left
  unreferenced (rule 13). HYDRATION-59's reasoning is preserved verbatim on the new prop's doc.
- the two cards' state branch, `briefCardState(rows.length, fetchError)`, one decision in
  brief-rows.ts, read by both cards. The Due-next card's `fetchError` foot note is removed because
  it is now that card's `failed` state rather than a second thing rendered beside an empty card, and
  the What-changed card gains the same state (it had none: a failed read rendered "Nothing added or
  updated in the last detection pass"). Copy and chrome are otherwise unchanged.

**Shared part touched: `StateNote`'s action target.** Both branches of the action rendered with
`padding: 0` and no height of their own, so the hit target was the text's ~13px line box, under
law-2's floor. Nothing had caught it because no ux smoke spec had ever mounted a StateNote CARRYING
an action; this lane's failure and empty states are the first, and they failed the rendering guard
at 375 and 1280 on their first run. The box is now `inline-flex`, `minHeight: 24` (law-2's small
branch, since a 44px control would be taller than the one-line strip the artboard draws). The type
treatment statenote.json pins (11px / 600) is untouched. This changes every StateNote action
sitewide, by ~11px of height on the strips that have one.

**Absence budget.** Unchanged, and now proven from the other side: mobfix61's one-token-per-row
precedence stays exactly as it is, and this lane adds the assertion that a brief row does not NEED
it, a change row that was carrying its one allowed token in four cells' worth of missing data is
now a full row, because the data is fetched rather than degraded.
## Lane duenext (2026-09-08), the two dead dashboard cards

| Date | Page / deviation | Detail | Ruled by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-08 | Dashboard "Due next" read 0 ITEMS while the corpus held 45 future-dated items | [CONFIRMED, live reads via Supabase MCP] The card is fed `get_workspace_intelligence_dashboard`, whose ORDER BY is `CASE effective_priority ... END, added_date DESC, id ASC LIMIT 50`: a slice chosen by PRIORITY, answering a different question from the one the card asks. Against org a0000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001 the active verified set is 1,433 rows, exactly 45 of which carry a future binding date under the rule the UI applies (`dueInfo`), and that slice held 23 of them. Migration 315 adds `get_workspace_due_next(p_org_id, p_limit)`, ordered by the binding date itself, bounded (default 24, clamped 1..100), workspace-scoped through `_workspace_active_items`. `src/app/page.tsx` unions its rows into the pool the SHARED selection function reads; `brief-rows.ts` is untouched. | Lane duenext; the card's own question |
| 2026-09-08 | ...and 5 of those 45 could never have rendered whatever the read returned | [CONFIRMED, source read] `mapWorkspaceItemRows` never set `Resource.complianceDeadline`, although every RPC it maps projects `ii.compliance_deadline` and `dueInfo` reads that field as one of its TWO date candidates. The field was undefined on every list and dashboard row in the app; only the single-item detail read ever populated it. Five live items have a future `compliance_deadline` and no future timeline milestone. One line in the mapper. | Lane duenext, rule 13 |
| 2026-09-08 | The due-next SQL deliberately ignores `entry_into_force` and `next_review_date` | [CONFIRMED, and stated rather than papered over] The brief describes the binding date as a coalesce across three columns. The UI's own rule uses two: `dueInfo`'s candidates are `r.complianceDeadline` and `r.timeline[].date`, and no Resource field carries either of the other two columns, so the UI cannot see them. A read selecting on them would return rows `buildDueNextRows` filters straight back out, which is the same defect class this lane exists to close. Widening what "due" means is a product decision; if it is ever taken, migration 315's `LEAST()` and `dueInfo`'s candidate list must widen in the SAME change. | Lane duenext; migration 315 header |
| 2026-09-08 | "What changed: no detection pass on record" was never a What-changed defect | [CONFIRMED, by live `integrity_flags` rows] `auditDate` is the empty string on exactly ONE payload, `fetchDashboardData`'s `emptyFallback`, and the empty string is the only input for which the card renders that sentence. So the card was reporting the payload, correctly. The payload was the fallback: `integrity_flags` carries rows at 2026-09-08T09:36:35Z and 2026-09-08T16:18:47Z, `subject_ref` "/", `created_by` "seed-fallback-trigger", description "Seed-fallback activated on /. Trigger: rpc_error." Meanwhile `get_workspace_recent_changes(org, 7)` matches 1,109 rows live and returns its 500-row cap. One defect, not two: both cards were blank for the same reason. | Lane duenext, rule 14 |
| 2026-09-08 | An empty result was being reported as a failed read | [CONFIRMED] `fetchDashboardData` ended `if (!resources.length) return { ...emptyFallback, _error, _fallbackTrigger: "rpc_error" }`. `fetchWorkspaceResources` collapsed "the RPC errored" and "the RPC returned nothing" into one empty return, so the caller could only guess, and it guessed "broken". That is what printed "Data temporarily unavailable. Refresh to retry." on a page that had not failed, and what put `Trigger: rpc_error` on the operator's platform-flags queue for events that were not RPC errors. The reader now reports `failed` explicitly; only a real failure returns the fallback, and a successful empty read returns the real payload with no `_error`, letting the surfaces render their own honest-empty states. This is the half about what the payload SAYS; lane rsc503's `refuseToCacheFallback` is the half about it not being CACHED. | Lane duenext; the Absence convention |
| 2026-09-08 | The audit date was fabricated on BOTH code paths | [CONFIRMED, measured] `fetchDashboardData` seeded `auditDate` with TODAY and then raised it by any changelog entry later than today, which no entry can be, so the card asserted "Detection pass <today>" every day whatever the system had done. Live: `item_changelog` holds 9 rows, newest `change_date` 2026-03-01, and the scrape cadence is held OFF per CLAUDE.md rule 16. `data.ts`'s failure factory did the same with a constant baked into the bundle. Both now take the date from evidence: newest changelog entry, else newest `added_date` in the What-changed feed, else the empty string, which is the honest "No detection pass on record". `src/data/audit-date.ts` lost its last importer and was deleted rather than left dormant (F25 caught it). | Lane duenext, rules 2 and 13 |
| 2026-09-08 | The audit specs measured that the cards EXIST, never that they have rows | [CONFIRMED] Every `compose-01-dashboard` row was a region-composition check, so a card whose read supplies nothing passed every assertion while rendering an empty state. That is exactly what shipped. Two rows added, one per card, asserting a non-zero row count against the populated fixture, and both proven by attack: with the fixture's future deadlines moved into the past the two rows go MATCH to NOT BUILT. | Lane duenext, rule 15 |

These seven rows became BLOCKING findings at this fold and not before for a reason worth stating:
lane cardrule moved every card in the product onto one `SectionCard`, so cards the guard could not
previously SEE as cards (a bare div, or an `<a>` with no chrome) now identify themselves. The rule
did not change and the pages did not change; the guard's eyesight did, which is the outcome a
shared part is supposed to produce.

| Date | Page | Deviation | Reason | Who ruled |
|---|---|---|---|---|

Everything else lane briefdata built is kept and is not duplicated: the by-id backfill of change rows (`fetchBriefResourcesByIds`, `mergeBriefCorpus`, `splitBriefIdsByShape`, `mergeBriefOverrides`), the `compliance_deadline` mapper line, the `briefCardState` model, the widened due-next window label, the watchlist cache guard and the membership fix. `src/app/page.tsx` now merges all three reads into ONE corpus. | FOLD 62, rule 13 |

Card.tsx's callers moved to it, its `noRule` became `suppressRuleForBandGrouping`, and its `data-guard-card` attribute moved onto SectionCard so the layout guard's L6/L10 still name a card that came from the shared shell. | FOLD 62, rule 13 |

That is operator item A3 exactly. It renders `SectionCard` now. The Tier definitions overlay in the same module carries a `fitness-allow: F42` marker with its reason, the convention the two other undesigned overlays already use. | FOLD 62, operator items A1/A3 |

### Two guards, one operator ruling

| Date | Page | Deviation | Reason | Who ruled |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-08 | (sitewide) | The layout guard's L9 hit-target floor now reads lane railfacets' dated desktop exemption | Operator item C1 puts the rail facet row at the artboard's 24px on desktop; the rendering guard's law-2 slot already carried a dated, component-scoped exemption for exactly that element above 768px, with the 44px touch target restored below 768 and measured at 390. L9 is a SECOND floor (44 long, 28 short) over the same element and fired 100 times on it the moment the two lanes met in one tree. Both guards read the SAME entry now, so the exemption cannot expire in one and live in the other, and the entry still covers only that one target, only at or above 768px, only until wave 70. Proven by attack in `layout-guard.test.mjs`: at 390, for any other control, for a name that merely begins the same way, and past the expiry wave, the finding returns. | FOLD 62, operator item C1 |
| 2026-09-08 | (sitewide) | `SectionHeading`'s aside wraps below 1280 instead of overflowing its card | [CONFIRMED, measured in chromium: the Due-next head was clientWidth 674, scrollWidth 740 at 1024] Below 1280 the rail stacks, so the content column is NARROWEST there on a desktop (674px against 778px at 1440), and the head had no rule in that band at all. Lane briefdata's window label states the span the selected rows actually cover, which is longer than the fixed "week of <date>" it replaced. Only the ASIDE wraps; the Anton title keeps `nowrap` at every width. The same edit gives MOBILE-60's own 390 wrap rule the `!important` it always needed: the aside sets `white-space: nowrap` INLINE, which beat the stylesheet rule, so that fix had never applied at any width. Nothing moves at 1440. | FOLD 62, operator L3 |

"Next due" is three ordered segments: still ahead soonest first, then already past most recent first, then undated. That cannot be expressed as one PostgREST `.order()`, and sorting a page after it was fetched sorts only that page, which is the same defect class as the 1000-row read cap train 61 closed. Each segment is exact-counted (`head: true`, no rows on the wire) and the page is cut against those counts with its own `.range()` per segment (`planRegisterPageSegments`). Overdue rows are KEPT, behind the upcoming ones: an obligation that has already bitten is not less relevant, it is simply not what "next due" means. | Operator item D2; coordinator note N4 |

Operator instruction for this lane, verbatim: "MARKET PAGE, MATCH THE ARTBOARD, NOT THE PROSE."
Every value below was read out of `id="p4"` in `Caros Ledge UI System.dc.html` and then MEASURED by
rendering that section itself in chromium, so the artboard column of this lane's region table is a
measurement and not an estimate. Where the brief's prose and the artboard's markup disagree, the
artboard wins and the disagreement is stated rather than quietly resolved.

| Date | Page / deviation | Detail | Ruled by |
|---|---|---|---|

The brief's prose estimates ~105px. The build's track is authored as `calc((100% - 40px) / 5)` so it IS that rule at any width: forcing the card to the artboard's own 778px re-resolved every track to 141.188px against a 746px content box, `(746-40)/5`. | Artboard over prose |
| 2026-09-08 | The headline value is Anton 17px, not the brief's "18px" | [CONFIRMED, measured] p4's value span computes to `font-size: 17px; line-height: 17.85px`. Built at 17px. | Artboard over prose |
| 2026-09-08 | p4's HEADLINE SERIES head carries a divider; the build does not | [CONFIRMED, and deliberately not matched] p4's head box is `padding:14px 16px 10px` with `border-bottom:1px solid rgba(0,0,0,.08)`. Operator ruling 5.1 (2026-09-07, CLOSED) says in as many words that there is NO divider below a section title. A standing ruling outranks the image it was written against, so the padding is p4's and the divider is not drawn. Recorded here and in compose-04-market-list.json's notes so it stays visible. | Ruling 5.1 over the artboard |
| 2026-09-08 | The remainder of the headline series scrolls sideways; p4 draws no scroller | [CONFIRMED] p4 draws exactly five cards and captions its head "10 of 16", so the image itself carries no overflow affordance and says nothing about where cards six to ten live. The horizontal scroll is the operator's own instruction for this lane. Built as `grid-auto-flow: column` + `overflow-x: auto` on p4's own track, so the first five are pixel-identical to the image at 1440 and the rest continue the same row. Below 768 the track stops dividing the viewport (a fifth of 390 is 60px, which cannot hold "EUR1,217/1000L") and becomes a fixed 150px card on the same sideways scroll. | Operator instruction; DP-1 for the 390 measure |
| 2026-09-08 | The headline delta's absence token is "pending", where p4 writes "BACKFILL" | [CONFIRMED] p4's EUR/USD card renders an em dash followed by a small-caps "BACKFILL" where its 1w move would be. The product's absence vocabulary is closed (`Absence.tsx`: connect data, not in primary source, pending, unscored) and does not contain that word, so the shape is p4's (dash plus small-caps reason) and the word is the vocabulary's. A bare dash was refused: it is indistinguishable from a real zero-change move. | Ruling 2.1's closed vocabulary over p4's wording |
| 2026-09-08 | The rail FILTERS caption is removed from /market and KEPT on /regulations | [CONFIRMED, both halves, by rendering the artboards] Artboard 04 draws NO caption under FILTERS; artboard 02 draws "Counts are live for the current selection. | 2026-09-08 | The rail's facet rows are 44px, where p4 draws 23px | [CONFIRMED, measured both sides, NOT changed] p4's facet row is `padding:4px 0` around a 13px box and 12.5px text and measures 23px. ## LANE SERIESFAMILY (2026-09-08): which series the headline row contains

| Date | Page / deviation | Detail | Ruled by |
